- **ion-identity-annotation-parsing**: Use when a feature quantification table, IIN edges file, or GNPS library (e.g., GNPS-IIMN-PROPOGATED) contains ion-identity annotation strings with inconsistent spellings or ordering that must be normalized before grouping, counting, or comparing adduct abundances. - **ion-identity-network-construction**: Use when you have an aligned LC-MS/MS feature list with consensus MS2 spectra and redundant unconnected nodes (adducts/isotopes of one compound), and you want MS1 ion-identity edges to propagate annotations in GNPS FBMN. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/ion-identity-network-construction))
- **ion-identity-network-group-counting**: Use when the analysis goal is to reproduce or verify the graph-level statistics of a GNPS FBMN job (feature count, IIN group count, node count, singleton count, edge count) from the deposited MZmine quant table and GNPS job ID. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/ion-identity-network-group-counting))
- **ion-identity-network-node-collapsing**: Use when an aligned feature table plus IIN graphml (ion groups keyed by shared IIN ID) is available and the goal is to reduce feature redundancy by merging ion-species nodes of the same compound into single representative molecular nodes. - **ion-identity-network-statistics-aggregation**: Use when you have IIMN (FBMN-IIN) outputs across multiple LC-MS datasets and need to summarize and compare the relative abundance of ion species/adducts per dataset or sample type. - **ion-identity-network-supplementary-edge-integration**: Use when you have an aligned feature list (feature quantification .csv + MS2 .mgf) plus IIN supplementary edge files and want to compare MS2-only FBMN against IIMN, or to merge redundant adduct/isotope subnetworks of the same compound. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/ion-identity-network-supplementary-edge-integration))
- **ion-image-augmentation-contrastive-learning**: Use when when you have preprocessed mass spectrometry ion images (single-channel 2D arrays or multi-channel spectral images) and need to train a self-supervised encoder to learn low-dimensional representations for downstream tasks such as co-localized ion discovery (COL mode) or isotope ion. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/ion-image-augmentation-contrastive-learning) · 2 copies)
- **ion-image-augmentation-design**: Use when when preparing ion image data from mass spectrometry imaging for contrastive self-supervised representation learning, and you need to generate augmented image pairs that reflect either co-localization relationships between different molecular ions (COL mode) or isotopic relationships. - **ion-image-augmentation-intensity-dependent**: Use when training a contrastive encoder on mass spectrometry imaging (MSI) data in ISO mode (isotope ions from the same molecule). - **ion-image-correlation-analysis**: Use when an rMSIproc peak matrix has matrix-related clusters (e.g. silver 'Ag1' annotation) whose S1 spectral and S2 morphological similarity scores fall below the user-specified threshold, so co-localized endogenous ions may be hiding inside the cluster. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/ion-image-embedding-optimization) · 3 copies)
- **ion-image-hotspot-truncation**: Use when raw MALDI-MSI pixel-by-ion matrices contain extreme intensity spikes (matrix crystal hotspots) that must be capped before self-supervised ion-image representation learning or any downstream similarity measurement.
